Remark 4.3
.

Recall that Kolmogorov’s three-series Theorem states that for a family
\{X_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
of independent random variables, the random series
\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}X_{m}
converges almost surely in
{\mathbb{R}}
if only if the following conditions hold for some
A>0
:

(i)

\displaystyle{\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|X_{m}\right|\geq A\right)<\infty.}

(ii)

\displaystyle{\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(X_{m}{\bf 1}_{\{|X_{m}|\leq A\}}\right)<\infty.}

(iii)

\displaystyle{\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{Var}\left(X_{m}{\bf 1}_{\{|X_{m}|\leq A\}}\right)<\infty.}

Lemma
4.2
guarantees the conditions for Kolmogorov’s three-series Theorem for the family of random variables
X_{m}:=\frac{\mathbb{X}(m)}{m}
in the case
A=1
. We thus conclude that
\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{X}(m)}{m}
converges almost surely in
{\mathbb{R}}
.

For any
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
, let

E_{p}(z):=\mathbb{E}\left(\left|1-\frac{\mathbb{X}(p)}{p}\right|^{-2z}\right).

Lemma 4.4
.

For
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
,

E_{p}(z)=1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle E_{p}(z)=\mathbb{E}\left(\left|1-\frac{\mathbb{X}(p)}{p}\right|^{-2z}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\left|1-\frac{\mathbb{X}(p)+\overline{\mathbb{X}}(p)}{p}+\frac{\mathbb{X}(p)\overline{\mathbb{X}}(p)}{p^{2}}\right|^{-z}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z},

and the statement follows.
∎

For any real number
r>0
, we define

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(r)

\displaystyle:=\log(\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X})|^{2r}))=\sum_{p}\log E_{p}(r),

(4.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r)

\displaystyle:=\log(\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X})|^{-2r}))=\sum_{p}\log E_{p}(-r).

Proposition 4.5
.

For any real number
r\geq 4
we have

(4.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(r)=2r\log\log r+2r\gamma+\frac{2r(C_{\max}-1)}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

(4.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r)=r\log\log r+r\gamma+\frac{r(C_{\min}-1)}{\log r}-r\log\zeta(3)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

and

(4.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(r)=2\log\log r+2\gamma+\frac{2C_{\max}}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),\\
&\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(r)=\log\log r+\left(\gamma-\log\zeta(3)\right)+\frac{C_{\min}}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),\end{split}

where

(4.7)

\displaystyle 2C_{\max}:=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{2e^{2t}-2e^{-t}}{t(e^{2t}+2e^{-t})}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{-6}{t(e^{3t}+2)}dt\approx 1.97455\ldots

and

(4.8)

\displaystyle C_{\min}:=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{2e^{t}-2e^{-2t}}{t(e^{-2t}+2e^{t})}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{-3e^{-2t}}{t(e^{-2t}+2e^{t})}dt\approx 1.40459\ldots.

Moreover, for all real numbers
y,t
such that
|y|\geq 3
and
|t|\leq|y|
we have

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(y)\asymp\frac{2}{|y|\log|y|}

\displaystyle\text{ and }\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}(y+it)\ll\frac{1}{|y|^{2}\log|y|}.

The same holds for the derivatives of
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r)
.

We will prove Proposition
4.5
in Section
4.2
.

Remark 4.6
.

The difference between the coefficients of
r\log\log r
in equations (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) reflects the distinction between the maximal values (achieved when

\mathbb{X}(p)=1
, and when the term
\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}
dominates in
E_{p}(r)
) and the minimal values (achieved when
\re(\mathbb{X}(p))=-\frac{1}{2}
, and when the term
\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}
dominates in
E_{p}(-r)
).

4.2.
Proof of Proposition
4.5

We first prove some preliminary results.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
r\geq 4
be a real number. Then we have

(4.10)

\log E_{p}(r)=\begin{cases}-2r\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+O(1)&\text{if}\;p<r^{\frac{2}{3}},\\
\log\left(\frac{e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}}{3}\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)&\text{if}\;p>r^{\frac{2}{3}},\end{cases}

and

\frac{E^{\prime}_{p}(r)}{E_{p}(r)}=\begin{cases}-2\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\left(1+O\left(\exp(-3r^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\right)&\text{ if }p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}},\\
\frac{\frac{2}{p}\left(e^{2r/p}-e^{-r/p}\right)}{e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)&\text{ if }p>r^{\frac{2}{3}}.\end{cases}

We also have

\log E_{p}(-r)=\begin{cases}r\log\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+O(1)&\text{ if }p<r^{\frac{2}{3}},\\
\log\left(\frac{e^{-2r/p}+2e^{r/p}}{3}\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)&\text{ if }p>r^{\frac{2}{3}},\end{cases}

and

\frac{E^{\prime}_{p}(-r)}{E_{p}(-r)}=\begin{cases}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\left(1+O\left(\exp(-3r^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\right)&\text{ if }p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}},\\
\frac{\frac{2}{p}\left(e^{r/p}-e^{-2r/p}\right)}{e^{-2r/p}+2e^{r/p}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)&\text{ if }p>r^{\frac{2}{3}}.\end{cases}

Proof.

We begin with (
4.10
). For
p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{p}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}\left(1+\frac{1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}}{\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}}\right)

(4.11)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}\left(1+O\left(\exp{(-3r^{\frac{1}{3}})}\right)\right),

since

\frac{1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}}{\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}}\sim 2\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-r}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-2r}\sim 2\exp(-3r/p).

This proves (
4.10
) when
p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}}
.
For
p>r^{\frac{2}{3}}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{p}(r)

\displaystyle=1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}

\displaystyle=1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{p}+O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\exp\left(-\frac{r}{p}+O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}-3\right)\exp\left(O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{3}\left(e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

and (
4.10
) follows for this case too.

Now we have

\displaystyle E_{p}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}\log\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)-\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).

For
p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}}
, we use (
4.11
) and get

\displaystyle\frac{E^{\prime}_{p}(r)}{E_{p}(r)}=

\displaystyle-\log\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\left(1+O\left(\exp(-3r^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\left(1+O\left(\exp(-3r^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\right).

For
p>r^{\frac{2}{3}}
, we get

\displaystyle E_{p}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}e^{2r/p}\left(1+O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)\left(-\frac{2}{p}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\right)-\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}e^{-r/p}\left(1+O\left(\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{p}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3p}\left(e^{2r/p}-e^{-r/p}\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3p}\left(e^{2r/p}-e^{-r/p}\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right).

This gives

\displaystyle\frac{E^{\prime}_{p}(r)}{E_{p}(r)}=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3p}\left(e^{2r/p}-e^{-r/p}\right)}{1+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}-3\right)}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{2}{p}\left(e^{2r/p}-e^{-r/p}\right)}{\left(e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}\right)}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{r}{p^{2}}\right)\right).

We now proceed to consider the
E_{p}(-r)
case.
For
p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{p}(-r)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{r}\left(1+\frac{1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{r}}{\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{r}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{r}\left(1+O\left(\exp{(-3r^{\frac{1}{3}})}\right)\right),

since

\frac{1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{r}}{\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{r}}\sim\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-2r}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-r}\sim\frac{1}{2}\exp(-3r/p).

The rest of the proof proceeds similarly as in the case
E_{p}(r)
.
∎

Define

(4.12)

\displaystyle f(t)=\begin{cases}\log\left(\frac{e^{2t}+2e^{-t}}{3}\right)&\text{ if }0\leq t<1,\\
\log\left(\frac{e^{2t}+2e^{-t}}{3}\right)-2t&\text{ if }1\leq t.\\
\end{cases}

and

(4.13)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}(t)=\begin{cases}\log\left(\frac{e^{-2t}+2e^{t}}{3}\right)&\text{ if }0\leq t<1,\\
\log\left(\frac{e^{-2t}+2e^{t}}{3}\right)-t&\text{ if }1\leq t.\\
\end{cases}

Lemma 4.8
.

The functions
f(t)
and
\widetilde{f}(t)
are bounded on
[0,\infty)
,
f(t)=t^{2}+t^{3}/3+O(t^{4})
and
\widetilde{f}(t)=t^{2}-t^{3}/3+O(t^{4})
if
0\leq t<1
. Moreover,

\widetilde{f}^{\prime}(t)=f^{\prime}(t)=\begin{cases}2t+O(t^{2})&\text{ if }0\leq t<1,\\
O(e^{-3t})&\text{ if }t\geq 1.\end{cases}

Proof.

We see that
f(t)
is bounded on
[0,\infty)
since
\frac{e^{2t}}{3}\leq\frac{e^{2t}+2e^{-t}}{3}\leq e^{2t}
, and the Taylor expansion gives
f(t)=t^{2}+t^{3}/3+O(t^{4})
if
0\leq t<1
.
For the derivative, if
0\leq t<1
, then

f^{\prime}(t)=\frac{2e^{2t}-2e^{-t}}{e^{2t}+2e^{-t}}=2t+O(t^{2})
,
and if
t\geq 1
, then

f^{\prime}(t)=\frac{-6e^{-t}}{e^{2t}+2e^{-t}}=O(e^{-3t})
. The proof is similar for
\widetilde{f}(t)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.5
.

We will first prove (
4.4
) and (
4.5
), beginning with (
4.4
).

Lemmas
4.7
and
4.8
give, as in
[
DL18
, Eq. (4.13)]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(r)=

\displaystyle-2r\sum_{p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}}}\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\sum_{p>r^{\frac{2}{3}}}\log\left(\frac{e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}}{3}\right)+O\left(r^{\frac{2}{3}}\right)

(4.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2r\sum_{p\leq r}\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\sum_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}<p<r^{\frac{4}{3}}}f\left(\frac{r}{p}\right)+O\left(r^{\frac{2}{3}}\right).

Using the Prime Number Theorem
\pi(t)-\mathrm{li}(t)\ll\frac{t}{(\log t)^{3}}
, together with partial summation, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}<p<r^{\frac{4}{3}}}f\left(\frac{r}{p}\right)=

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}}^{r^{\frac{4}{3}}}f\left(\frac{r}{t}\right)\frac{dt}{\log t}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{r}{\log r}\int_{r^{-1/3}}^{r^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{f(u)}{u^{2}}du+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right).

We have

\int_{r^{-1/3}}^{r^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{f(u)}{u^{2}}du=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f(u)}{u^{2}}du+O\left(r^{-1/3}\right),

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f(u)}{u^{2}}du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{\prime}(u)}{u}du-\left(\lim_{x\to 1^{-}}\frac{f(x)}{x}-\lim_{x\to 0^{+}}\frac{f(x)}{x}+\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{f(x)}{x}-\lim_{x\to 1^{+}}\frac{f(x)}{x}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{\prime}(u)}{u}du-2

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{2e^{2t}-2e^{-t}}{t(e^{2t}+2e^{-t})}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{-6}{t(e^{3t}+2)}dt-2=2C_{\max}-2.

Then, collecting the above estimates and replacing in (
4.14
) gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(r)=

\displaystyle-2r\sum_{p\leq r}\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\frac{r}{\log r}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{\prime}(u)}{u}du-2\frac{r}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2r\log\log r+2r\gamma+\frac{2r(C_{\max}-1)}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

where we have used the explicit form of Mertens’ third theorem

\prod_{p\leq r}\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)=\frac{e^{-\gamma}}{\log{r}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{3}{r}}\right)

due to Rosser and Schoenfeld
[
RS62
]
.

We now proceed to (
4.5
), the proof is very similar to (
4.4
) but we include the details as we arrive at a different constant.

Lemmas
4.7
and
4.8
give, as in the case
\mathcal{L}(r)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r)=

\displaystyle r\sum_{p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}}}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+\sum_{p>r^{\frac{2}{3}}}\log\left(\frac{e^{-2r/p}+2e^{r/p}}{3}\right)+O\left(r^{\frac{2}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r\sum_{p\leq r}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+\sum_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}<p<r^{\frac{4}{3}}}\widetilde{f}\left(\frac{r}{p}\right)+O\left(r^{\frac{2}{3}}\right),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}<p<r^{\frac{4}{3}}}\widetilde{f}\left(\frac{r}{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{\log r}\int_{r^{-1/3}}^{r^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{\widetilde{f}(u)}{u^{2}}du+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right).

We have

\int_{r^{-1/3}}^{r^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{\widetilde{f}(u)}{u^{2}}du=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\widetilde{f}(u)}{u^{2}}du+O\left(r^{-1/3}\right),

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\widetilde{f}(u)}{u^{2}}du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\widetilde{f}^{\prime}(u)}{u}du-\left(\lim_{x\to 1^{-}}\frac{\widetilde{f}(x)}{x}-\lim_{x\to 0^{+}}\frac{\widetilde{f}(x)}{x}+\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{f}(x)}{x}-\lim_{x\to 1^{+}}\frac{\widetilde{f}(x)}{x}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{2e^{t}-2e^{-2t}}{t(e^{-2t}+2e^{t})}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{-3e^{-2t}}{t(e^{-2t}+2e^{t})}dt-1=C_{\min}-1.

Then, collecting the above estimates gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r)=

\displaystyle r\sum_{p\leq r}\left(\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{3}}\right)-\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\right)+\frac{r}{\log r}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\widetilde{f}^{\prime}(u)}{u}du-\frac{r}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r\log\log r+r\gamma-r\log\zeta(3)+\frac{r(C_{\min}-1)}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

where we have applied Mertens’ third theorem as before.

We will only prove the first equation in (
4.6
) as the proofs are identical for both. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(r)=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{p\leq r^{\frac{2}{3}}}\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\sum_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}<p}\frac{2\left(e^{2r/p}-e^{-r/p}\right)}{p\left(e^{2r/p}+2e^{-r/p}\right)}+O\left(r^{-1/3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{p\leq r}\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\sum_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}<p<r^{\frac{4}{3}}}\frac{1}{p}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{p}\right)+O\left(r^{-1/3}\right),

and

\sum_{r^{\frac{2}{3}}<p<r^{\frac{4}{3}}}\frac{1}{p}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{p}\right)=\frac{1}{\log r}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{\prime}(u)}{u}du+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

which gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(r)=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{p\leq r}\log\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\frac{1}{\log r}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{\prime}(u)}{u}du+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\log\log r+2\gamma+\frac{2C_{\max}}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log r)^{2}}\right).

The approach for the results in (
4.9
) is the same, although the calculations are more technical.
∎

The following result corresponds to Lemma 4.5 from
[
DL18
]
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
r
be large. If
p\gg r
, then for some positive constant
b_{1}
we have

\frac{|E_{p}(r+it)|}{E_{p}(r)},\frac{|E_{p}(-r+it)|}{E_{p}(-r)}\leq\exp\left(-b_{1}\left(1-\cos\left(t\log\left(\frac{p^{2}+p+1}{p^{2}-2p+1}\right)\right)\right)\right).

Proof.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}
be positive real numbers and
\theta_{2},\theta_{3}
be real numbers. The following inequality is established in the proof of
[
GS03
, Lemma 3.2]
:

|x_{1}+x_{2}e^{i\theta_{2}}+x_{3}e^{i\theta_{3}}|\leq(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})\exp\left(-\frac{x_{1}x_{3}(1-\cos\theta_{3})}{(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})^{2}}\right).

We apply the above inequality to
x_{1}=\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}
,
x_{2}=1-\alpha_{p}
,
x_{3}=\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-r}
and
\theta_{2}=t\log\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)
, and
\theta_{3}=t\log\left(\frac{p^{2}-2p+1}{p^{2}+p+1}\right)
. We conclude since
\cos(\log(x))=\cos(\log(1/x))
. The proof for
-r
is completely analogous.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
r
be large. Then, there exists a constant
b_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{r+it}\right)\right|}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{r}\right)},\frac{\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{-r+it}\right)\right|}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{-r}\right)}\ll\begin{cases}\exp\left(-b_{2}\frac{t^{2}}{r\log r}\right)&\text{if $|t|\leq r/4$,}\\
\exp\left(-b_{2}\frac{|t|}{\log|t|}\right)&\text{if $|t|>r/4$.}\end{cases}

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.9
with exactly the same proof as in
[
DL18
, Lemma 4.6]
. ∎

Lemma 4.11
.

For any
\lambda>0
, let

(4.15)

\displaystyle f(y)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(c)}y^{s}\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\frac{ds}{s}.

Then,

\displaystyle f(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{\lambda}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(c)}(ye^{u})^{s}\frac{ds}{s}\right)du=\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{ if }y>1,\\
1+\frac{\log y}{\lambda},&\mbox{ if }e^{-\lambda}\leq y\leq 1,\\
0,&\mbox{ if }y<e^{-\lambda},\end{cases}

and we have

\displaystyle f(ye^{-\lambda})\leq\phi(y)\leq f(y),

where
\phi(y)
is the indicator function

\phi(y):=\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if $y>0$},\\
0,&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

We will prove Theorem
4.1
with the saddle-point technique, from which we deduce that
\Phi(\tau)
is given by a formula involving
{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2r})
evaluated at the saddle point

\kappa=\kappa(\tau)
defined below. For
\Psi(\tau)
, the distribution is related to
{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{-2r})
evaluated at the saddle point
\tilde{\kappa}=\tilde{\kappa}(\tau)
.

Let
\tau
be a large real number and consider the differential equations

(4.16)

\left(\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X})|^{2r})(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2r}\right)^{\prime}=0\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(r)=2(\log\tau+\gamma),

and

(4.17)

\left(\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X})|^{-2r})\left(\frac{e^{\gamma}\tau^{2}}{\zeta(3)}\right)^{-r}\right)^{\prime}=0\Leftrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(r)=2\log\tau+\gamma-\log\zeta(3),

where the derivative is taken with respect to the real variable
r
. It follows from (
4.6
) that
\lim_{r\to\infty}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(r)=\infty
. Moreover, a straightforward calculation shows that

(4.18)

\displaystyle E^{\prime\prime}_{p}(r)E_{p}(r)>(E^{\prime}_{p}(r))^{2}

for all primes
p
, so that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(r)>0.
The same calculation leads to (
4.18
)
with
r
replaced
-r
, and then we also have that

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(r)>0
. Thus, we deduce that (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) have unique solutions
\kappa:=\kappa(\tau)
and
\widetilde{\kappa}:=\widetilde{\kappa}(\tau)
respectively.

We write
\Phi(\tau)
as

\Phi(\tau)={\mathbb{P}}\left((|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2}/(e^{\gamma}\tau))^{2}>1\right).

Let
A=\frac{|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2}}{e^{2\gamma}\tau^{2}}>1
be an event. Then

\displaystyle\phi(A)=\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if the event $A$ occurs},\\
0,&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

is a random variable and
{\mathbb{E}}(\phi(A))=\Phi(\tau)={\mathbb{P}}(A).

By Lemma
4.11
, where
f
is the function defined by
(
4.15
), we have

(4.19)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}(f(Ae^{-\lambda}))\leq{\mathbb{E}}(\phi(A))=\Phi(\tau)\leq{\mathbb{E}}(f(A)),

since
X\leq Y
implies that
{\mathbb{E}}(X)\leq{\mathbb{E}}(Y)
.
Using the definition of
f
and
A
, we write

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}(f(Ae^{-\lambda}))

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(c)}\frac{|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}}{(e^{2\gamma}\tau^{2})^{s}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(c)}\frac{\mathbb{E}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s})}{(e^{2\gamma}\tau^{2})^{s}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s},

where
0<\lambda<1/(2\kappa)
is a real number to the chosen later. With the above equation and (
4.19
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\kappa)}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s})}{(e^{2\gamma}\tau^{2})^{s}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\Phi(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\kappa)}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s})}{(e^{2\gamma}\tau^{2})^{s}}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s},

and it follows that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\kappa-i\infty}^{\kappa+i\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s}-\Phi(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\kappa-i\infty}^{\kappa+i\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\left(\frac{1-e^{-\lambda s}}{s}\right)ds.

We remark that since
\lambda\kappa<\frac{1}{2}
by the choice of
\lambda
, we have
|e^{\lambda s}-1|<3
and
|e^{-\lambda s}-1|<2
.
For
T\ll\kappa

(to be chosen later), using Lemma
4.10
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\kappa+iT}^{\kappa+i\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2\kappa}}{\lambda}\left(\int_{T}^{\kappa/2}\exp\left(-b_{2}\frac{t^{2}}{\kappa\log\kappa}\right)\frac{dt}{\kappa^{2}+t^{2}}+\int_{\kappa/2}^{\infty}\exp\left(-b_{2}\frac{t}{\log t}\right)\frac{dt}{\kappa^{2}+t^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2\kappa}}{\lambda}\left(\frac{e^{-c_{1}\frac{T^{2}}{\kappa\log\kappa}}}{T}+\frac{e^{-c_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\log\kappa}}}{\kappa}\right),

with some absolute constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
.

Furthermore, when
|t|\leq T
, and
s=\kappa+it
then
|(1-e^{-\lambda s})(e^{\lambda s}-1)|\ll\lambda^{2}|s|^{2}
, and we have

\displaystyle\int_{\kappa-iT}^{\kappa+iT}{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\left(\frac{1-e^{-\lambda s}}{s}\right)ds\ll\lambda T{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2\kappa},

which gives

(4.20)

\displaystyle\Phi(\tau)-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\kappa-iT}^{\kappa+iT}{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2s}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle\ll\left(\frac{e^{-c_{1}\frac{T^{2}}{\kappa\log\kappa}}}{\lambda T}+\frac{e^{-c_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\log\kappa}}}{\lambda\kappa}+\lambda T\right){\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa}\right)(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2\kappa}.

We now give a bound for the integral between
\kappa-iT
and
\kappa+iT
.
It follows from (
4.9
) of Proposition
4.5
that when
|t|\leq T
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\kappa+it)=\mathcal{L}(\kappa)+it\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\kappa)-\frac{t^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}{2}+O\left(\frac{|t|^{3}}{\kappa^{2}\log{\kappa}}\right).

We also have

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s^{2}}=\frac{1}{s}\left(1+O\left(\lambda\kappa\right)\right)=\frac{1}{\kappa}\left(1-i\frac{t}{\kappa}+O\left(\lambda\kappa+\frac{t^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)\right).

Writing
{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)=\exp\left(\mathcal{L}(s)\right)
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\kappa)=2(\log{\tau}+\gamma)
, and using the above two equations, we have

\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)}{(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2s}}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s^{2}}\right)=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa})}{\kappa(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2\kappa}}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)\right)\left(1-i\frac{t}{\kappa}+O\left(\lambda\kappa+\frac{t^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}+\frac{|t|^{3}}{\kappa^{2}\log\kappa}\right)\right),

since

\displaystyle(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2s}\exp{\left(it\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\kappa)\right)}=(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2\kappa-2it}\exp{\left(2it\log\tau\right)}\exp{\left(2it\gamma\right)}=(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{-2\kappa}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\kappa-iT}^{\kappa+iT}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)}{(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2s}}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\;\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa})}{\kappa(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2\kappa}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-T}^{T}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)\right)\left(1-i\frac{t}{\kappa}+O\left(\lambda\kappa+\frac{t^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}+\frac{|t|^{3}}{\kappa^{2}\log\kappa}\right)\right)dt.

Since
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)\asymp 1/(\kappa\log{\kappa})
by (
4.9
) of Proposition
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-T}^{T}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)\right)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}}\left(1+O\left(e^{-c_{3}T^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\int_{-T}^{T}|t|^{n}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)\right)dt

\displaystyle\ll\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}}\hskip 28.45274pt1\leq n\leq 3,

and replacing above, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\kappa-iT}^{\kappa+iT}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2s}\right)}{(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2s}}\left(\frac{e^{\lambda s}-1}{\lambda s}\right)\frac{ds}{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa})}{\kappa\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2\kappa}}\left(1+O\left(\lambda\kappa+\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}+\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}\log\kappa\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)^{\frac{3}{2}}}+e^{-c_{3}T^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}\right)\right),

for some absolute constant
c_{3}
.
Replacing in (
4.20
), and using again (
4.9
) of Proposition
4.5
to get

\kappa\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}\ll\frac{\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(\log{\kappa})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

we finally obtain

\displaystyle\Phi(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa})}{\kappa\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2\kappa}}\left(1+O\left(\lambda\kappa+\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}+\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}\log\kappa\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)^{\frac{3}{2}}}+e^{-c_{3}T^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{e^{-c_{1}\frac{T^{2}}{\kappa\log\kappa}}}{\lambda T}\frac{\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(\log\kappa)^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{e^{-c_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\log\kappa}}}{\lambda\kappa}\frac{\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(\log\kappa)^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\lambda T\frac{\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(\log\kappa)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right).

We choose
T=\kappa^{\delta}
for any
1/2<\delta<1
and
\lambda=\kappa^{-2}
, which can be done by taking
\tau
sufficiently large by (
4.21
). We conclude that

\Phi(\tau)=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa})}{\kappa\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2\kappa}}\left(1+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\kappa}{\kappa}}\right)\right).

The proof is similar for
\Psi(\tau)
. This completes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

∎

4.3.
Proofs of Theorems
1.2
and
1.3
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Recall that
{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2r}\right)=\exp\left(\mathcal{L}(r)\right)
, and from Proposition
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(r)

\displaystyle=2r\log\log r+2\gamma r+\frac{2r(C_{\max}-1)}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=2\log\log r+2\gamma+\frac{2C_{\max}}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log r)^{2}}\right).

Recall that
\kappa=\kappa(\tau)
is the unique solution to
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\kappa)=2(\log\tau+\gamma)
. Therefore,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\log\tau=\log\log\kappa+\frac{C_{\max}}{\log\kappa}+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log\kappa)^{2}}\right).

Using these estimates and Theorem
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2\kappa})}{\kappa\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}(e^{\gamma}\tau)^{2\kappa}}\left(1+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\kappa}{\kappa}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\mathcal{L}(\kappa)-2\kappa(\log\tau+\gamma)+O(\log\kappa)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2\kappa}{\log\kappa}+O\left(\frac{\kappa}{(\log\kappa)^{2}}\right)\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O(\tau^{-1})\right)\right),

since
\log\kappa=\tau-C_{\max}+O(\tau^{-1})
from (
4.21
).

The proof is similar for
\Psi(\tau)
by noting that

{\mathbb{E}}\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{-2r}\right)=\exp\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r)\right)
, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r)

\displaystyle=r\log\log r+r\gamma+\frac{r(C_{\min}-1)}{\log r}-r\log\zeta(3)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=\log\log r+(\gamma-\log{\zeta(3)})+\frac{C_{\min}}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log r)^{2}}\right).

Let
\widetilde{\kappa}
be the unique solution to
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\kappa})=2\log\tau+\gamma-\log\zeta(3))
.
Then,

(4.22)

\displaystyle 2\log\tau=\log\log\tilde{\kappa}+\frac{C_{\min}}{\log\tilde{\kappa}}+O\left(\frac{1}{(\log\tilde{\kappa})^{2}}\right).

Using these estimates and Theorem
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Psi(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{-2\tilde{\kappa}})\zeta(3)^{\tilde{\kappa}}}{\tilde{\kappa}\sqrt{2\pi\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(\kappa)}(e^{\gamma}\tau^{2})^{\tilde{\kappa}}}\left(1+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\tilde{\kappa}}{\tilde{\kappa}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\tilde{\kappa})-\tilde{\kappa}(2\log\tau+\gamma-\log\zeta(3))+O(\log\tilde{\kappa})\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{\tilde{\kappa}}{\log\tilde{\kappa}}+O\left(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}}{(\log\tilde{\kappa})^{2}}\right)\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{e^{\tau^{2}-C_{\min}}}{\tau^{2}}\left(1+O(\tau^{-1})\right)\right),

since
\log\tilde{\kappa}=\tau^{2}-C_{\min}+O(\tau^{-2})
from (
4.22
).

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We adapt the proof of
[
GS06
, Theorem 1]
to our context.
For any
r\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle 2r\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{2r}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\int_{0}^{|L(1,\chi)|e^{-\gamma}}t^{2r-1}dt=\frac{e^{-2r\gamma}}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}|L(1,\chi)|^{2r}.

Then, by Theorem
3.1
, uniformly for
r\leq\frac{\log X}{16\log_{2}X\log_{3}X}
, we get

\displaystyle 2r\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt=e^{-2\gamma r}E\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2r}\right)+e^{-2\gamma r}\exp\left(-\frac{\log X}{16\log_{2}X}\right),

and by Proposition
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle E\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}})|^{2r}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(2r\log\log r+2r\gamma+\frac{2r(C_{\max}-1)}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=(\log r)^{2r}e^{2\gamma r}\exp\left(\frac{2r(C_{\max}-1)}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right).

This implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{(\log r)^{2r}}{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{2r}\exp\left(-2\gamma r-\frac{\log X}{16\log_{2}X}\right)

(4.23)

\displaystyle=(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right).

Let
r_{2}=re^{\delta}
, where
\delta>0
is sufficiently small to be determined later. We apply (
4.3
) with
r_{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt

\displaystyle\leq(\tau+\delta)^{2r-2r_{2}}\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r_{2}-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt\leq(\tau+\delta)^{2r-2r_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r_{2}-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt

\displaystyle=(\tau+\delta)^{2r(1-e^{\delta})}(\log r+\delta)^{2re^{\delta}}\exp\left(\frac{2re^{\delta}}{\log r+\delta}(C_{\max}-1)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{2r(1-e^{\delta})}(\log r)^{2re^{\delta}}\exp\left(2r(1-e^{\delta})\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\tau}\right)+2re^{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\log r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{2re^{\delta}}{\log r}\left(C_{\max}-1\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=(\log{r})^{2r}\exp\left(2r(1-e^{\delta})\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\tau}\right)+2re^{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\log r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{2re^{\delta}}{\log r}\left(C_{\max}-1\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right)\exp\left(2r(1-e^{\delta})\left(\log{\tau}-\log_{2}{r}\right)\right),

which holds uniformly for

(4.24)

\displaystyle r_{2}\leq\frac{\log X}{16\log_{2}X\log_{3}X}\iff r\leq e^{-\delta}\frac{\log X}{16\log_{2}X\log_{3}X}.

We now choose
r=r(\tau)
by
\log{r}=\tau-C_{\max}
. This gives

\displaystyle\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt

\displaystyle\leq(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(2r(1-e^{\delta})\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\tau}\right)+2re^{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\log r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log{r}}\left(C_{\max}-e^{\delta}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log{r}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}\left(C_{\max}+\delta-e^{\delta}\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right).

Using
\delta=\frac{c}{\sqrt{\log r}}
in the last equation, and the fact that
1+\delta-e^{\delta}=-\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}+O(\delta^{3})
, the last line becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt

\displaystyle\leq(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}\left(C_{\max}-1\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right)\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right)

adjusting the constant
c
. Using (
4.3
), we have proven that

(4.25)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt\right)\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right),

and similarly, we can prove that

(4.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau-\delta}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt\right)\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right).

The exponential decay of (
4.25
) and (
4.26
) implies that most of the mass of the integral is between
\tau-\delta
and
\tau+\delta
when
r
grows. More precisely, again using (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\tau-\delta}^{\tau+\delta}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt\left(1+O\left(\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\times\exp\left(\log\left(1+O\left(\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right)\right)\right)\right)

(4.27)

\displaystyle=(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)\right).

We have

\int_{\tau-\delta}^{\tau+\delta}t^{2r-1}dt=\frac{(\tau+\delta)^{2r}-(\tau-\delta)^{2r}}{2r}=\tau^{2r}\exp\left(O\left(\frac{\delta r}{\tau}\right)\right),

and since
\phi_{X}(t)={\mathbb{P}}\left(|L(1,\chi)|>e^{\gamma}t\right)
is non-increasing, this gives

\tau^{2r}\exp\left(O\left(\frac{\delta r}{\tau}\right)\right)\phi_{X}(\tau+\delta)\leq\int_{\tau-\delta}^{\tau+\delta}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t)dt\leq\tau^{2r}\exp\left(O\left(\frac{\delta r}{\tau}\right)\right)\phi_{X}(\tau-\delta).

Replacing the middle term by (
4.27
), we have

\phi_{X}(\tau+\delta)\leq\left(\frac{\log r}{\tau}\right)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{\delta r}{\tau}\right)\right)\leq\phi_{X}(\tau-\delta).

Since
\tau=\log r+C_{\max}\iff r=e^{\tau-C_{\max}}
, we have that the middle expression above is

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)+2r\log\left(1-\frac{C_{\max}}{\tau}\right)+O\left(\frac{\delta r}{\tau}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)-\frac{2rC_{\max}}{\tau}+O\left(\frac{r}{\tau^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{\delta r}{\tau}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2r}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{\tau^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{\delta r}{\tau}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\delta\right)+O(1/\tau)\right)\right).

Therefore,

\phi_{X}(\tau+\delta)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\delta\right)\right)\right)\leq\phi_{X}(\tau-\delta),

and we have

\phi_{X}(\tau)=\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right)\right).

Recalling (
4.24
), this holds uniformly in the range

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\log r+C_{\max}\leq\log\left(e^{-\delta}\frac{\log X}{16\log_{2}X\log_{3}X}\right)+C_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}X-\log_{3}X-\log_{4}X-2.

A similar estimate holds for
\psi_{X}(\tau)
.
∎

5.
Extreme values of
|L(1,\chi)|

In this section, we investigate extreme values of
|L(1,\chi)|
(under GRH). Our first goal is to prove Proposition
1.7
, exhibiting the upper and lower bounds for characters of prime order
\ell\geq 3

following the work of Littlewood
[
Lit28
]
for quadratic characters.

We will focus on the lower bound, since the upper bound can be obtained by the same arguments given by Littlewood.
We first recall the following result.

Lemma 5.1
.

[
Lit28
, Lemma 7]

Let
\chi
be a non-principal character of conductor
q
. Assume GRH for
L(s,\chi)
. Then, as
y\rightarrow 0
,

(5.1)

\displaystyle-\log|L(1,\chi)|=-\re\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Lambda_{1}(n)\chi(n)}{n}e^{-ny}\right)+O_{\epsilon}(y^{1/2-\epsilon}\log q)+o(1),

where
\Lambda_{1}(n)=\frac{\Lambda(n)}{\log n}
.

Proof of Proposition
1.7
.

To prove the lower bound, we follow the proof of
[
Lit28
, Theorem 1]
. Taking
y=(\log q)^{-2-7\epsilon}
and
x=(\log q)^{2+8\epsilon}
in (
5.1
), we have that

O_{\epsilon}(y^{1/2-\epsilon}\log q)=o(1)\qquad\mbox{ and }\qquad\sum_{n\geq x}\frac{\Lambda_{1}(n)\chi(n)}{n}e^{-ny}=o(1),

and we obtain

\displaystyle-\log|L(1,\chi)|

\displaystyle=-\re\left(\sum_{p^{m}\leq x}\frac{\Lambda_{1}(p^{m})\chi(p^{m})}{p^{m}}e^{-p^{m}y}\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=-\re\left(\sum_{p\leq x}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}e^{-py}\right)-\re\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p^{m}\leq x\\
m>1\end{subarray}}\frac{\chi(p)^{m}}{mp^{m}}e^{-p^{m}y}\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{p\leq x}\left(\frac{\re(\chi(p))}{p}e^{-py}+\sum_{m=2}^{M_{p}}\frac{\re(\chi(p))^{m})}{mp^{m}}\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=:-\sum_{p\leq x}A_{p}+o(1),

where
M_{p}=[\log x/\log p]
and we have used the fact that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p^{m}\leq x\\
m>1\end{subarray}}\frac{1-e^{-p^{m}y}}{mp^{m}}=O(yx^{\frac{1}{2}})=o(1).

Note that
\chi(p)\in\{0,1,\omega_{\ell},\dots,\omega_{\ell}^{\ell-1}\}
. If
\chi(p)=0
or 1, then
A_{p}\geq 0
.
Let
\chi(p)=\omega_{\ell}^{h}
. Then
\chi(p)^{m}=\omega_{\ell}^{hm}
for any
m\geq 1
. Thus,

\displaystyle A_{p}=

\displaystyle\frac{(\omega_{\ell}^{h}+\omega_{\ell}^{-h})(e^{-py}-1)}{2p}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\omega_{\ell}^{h}+\omega_{\ell}^{-h}}{p}+\frac{\omega_{\ell}^{2h}+\omega_{\ell}^{-2h}}{2p^{2}}+\frac{\omega_{\ell}^{3h}+\omega_{\ell}^{-3h}}{3p^{3}}-\cdots\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\cos\left(\frac{2\pi h}{\ell}\right)(e^{-py}-1)}{p}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(1-\frac{2\cos\left(\frac{2\pi h}{\ell}\right)}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).

When
\cos\left(\frac{2\pi h}{\ell}\right)<0
, the first term above is positive, and we can bound
A_{p}
below by the second term involving the
\log
.
Otherwise, notice that
\frac{e^{-py}-1}{p}\geq-y=-(\log q)^{-2+7\epsilon}
. Summing over
p
, we obtain

-\log|L(1,\chi)|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p\leq x}\log\left(1-\frac{2\cos\left(\frac{2\pi h}{\ell}\right)}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+O\left((\log q)^{-2+7\epsilon}\right).

We remark that, since
\ell
is odd, the real part of
\omega_{\ell}^{h}
is minimized when
h=\frac{\ell-1}{2}
or
h=\frac{\ell+1}{2}
. In other words,

-\cos\left(\frac{2\pi h}{\ell}\right)\leq-\cos\left(\frac{\pi(\ell-1)}{\ell}\right)=\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p\leq x}\log\left(1-\frac{2\cos\left(\frac{2\pi h}{\ell}\right)}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\log\prod_{p\leq x}\left(1+\frac{2\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\log\prod_{p\leq x}\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-2\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}+\frac{1}{2}\log\prod_{p\leq x}\left[\left(1+\frac{2\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{2\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)\log\left(e^{\gamma}\log x\right)+\log\left(1+O\left(\frac{\log x}{\sqrt{x}}\right)\right)-\log\left(C_{\ell}\right),

where

C_{\ell}=\prod_{p}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}\left(1+\frac{2\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}\right],

and we have applied Mertens’ third theorem (with the error term coming from assuming RH). Taking the exponential and recalling our choice of
x=(\log q)^{2+5\epsilon}
, we conclude that

|L(1,\chi)|\geq\frac{C_{\ell}}{\left(2e^{\gamma}\log\log q\right)^{\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\log_{2}q}{\log q}\right)\right).

∎

To prove the
\Omega
-results, we need the following auxiliary statements.

Proposition 5.2
.

Assume GRH for Hecke
L
-functions over
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{3})
. Let
P(z)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}p^{2}=e^{2z+o(z)}.
Let
\epsilon_{p}\in\{1,\omega_{3},\omega_{3}^{2}\}
for each rational prime
p\leq z
, and let

\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)=\{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)\,|\,\mathrm{cond}(\chi)\text{ is prime and }\chi(p)=\epsilon_{p}\text{ for each }p\leq z\}.

Then, for
z\ll X^{\frac{1}{6}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}+O(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP(z)),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}L(1,\chi)\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}\zeta(3)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-2}}{p}+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-1}}{p^{2}}\right)+O(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{3}(XP(z))).

Proof.

By Lemma
3.7
, studying
\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)
is equivalent to studying
\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)

\displaystyle=\left\{\mathfrak{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]\setminus{\mathbb{Z}}:\mathfrak{q}\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3),\mathfrak{q}\text{ is prime, }N(\mathfrak{q})\leqslant X\text{ and }\left(\frac{p}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}=\epsilon_{p}\text{ for each }p\leq z\right\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)

\displaystyle=\#\left\{\mathfrak{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]:\mathfrak{q}\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3),\mathfrak{q}\text{ is prime, }N(\mathfrak{q})\leqslant X\text{ and }\left(\frac{p}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}=\epsilon_{p}\text{ for each }p\leq z\right\}

\displaystyle+O\left(\#\left\{q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}:q\equiv 2\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3),q\text{ is prime, }N(q)\leqslant X\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\#\left\{\mathfrak{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]:\mathfrak{q}\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3),\mathfrak{q}\text{ is prime, }N(\mathfrak{q})\leqslant X\text{ and }\left(\frac{p}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}=\epsilon_{p}\text{ for each }p\leq z\right\}

(5.2)

\displaystyle+O\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

We first write a characteristic function for
\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)
. For a rational
m\mid P(z)
, we define
\epsilon_{m}=\prod_{p\mid m}\epsilon_{p}^{v_{p}(m)}.
Then,

\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\left(\frac{m}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\mathfrak{q}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)$},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We write

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathfrak{q}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}\log N(\mathfrak{q})=\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]\setminus{\mathbb{Z}}\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\leq X\\
\mathfrak{q}\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
\mathfrak{q}\text{ is prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{m}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}\log{N(\mathfrak{q})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\leq X\\
\mathfrak{q}\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
\mathfrak{q}\text{ is prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{m}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}\log{N(\mathfrak{q})}+O(X^{\frac{1}{2}}),

by (
5
). The main term comes from
m=1
and, by the Prime Number Theorem, it equals
\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}
with an error term of
O(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log X)
if we assume GRH. When
m\neq 1
, then
\left(\frac{m}{\cdot}\right)_{3}
is a non-trivial character modulo at most
9m
since
m
is not a cube, and using the Chebotarev density theorem under GRH, we have that the sum over
\mathfrak{q}
is
O(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP(z)^{2}))
. This gives the first statement.

For the second result, first note that

(5.3)

L(1,\chi)=\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{\chi(n)}{n}+\sum_{n>N}\frac{\chi(n)}{n}=\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{\chi(n)}{n}+O\left(\frac{\mathrm{cond}(\chi)}{N}\right),

where

(5.4)

\displaystyle N:=X^{3/2}P(z)^{3/2}.

Thus we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}L(1,\chi)\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)\left(\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{\chi(n)}{n}+O\left(\frac{\mathrm{cond}(\chi)}{N}\right)\right)

(5.5)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\leq X\\
\mathfrak{q}\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
\mathfrak{q}\text{ is prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{nm}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}\log{N(\mathfrak{q})}+O\left(\frac{X^{2}\log X}{N}\right).

The character
\left(\frac{nm}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{3}
is principal only when

is to have

, and we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{n_{3}\leq N^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{1}{n_{3}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{2}\leq(N/n_{3}^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
n_{2}\mid\widehat{P}(z)\end{subarray}}\frac{\epsilon_{n_{2}}^{-1}}{n_{2}^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq N/n_{2}^{2}n_{3}^{3}\\
(n_{2},n_{1})=1\\
n_{1}\mid\widehat{P}(z)\end{subarray}}\frac{\epsilon_{n_{1}}^{-2}}{n_{1}}.

The condition
\widehat{P}(z)\leq N/n_{2}^{3}n_{3}^{3}
is equivalent to
n_{2}^{2}n_{3}^{3}\leq N/\widehat{P}(z)=X^{3/2}P(z)
with the choice of
N
given by (
5.4
). In this case, we have no condition on the size of
n_{1}
in the inside sum, and we compute the above sum as

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{p\leq z}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-2}}{p}\right)\sum_{n_{3}\leq N^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{1}{n_{3}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{2}\leq{(N/n_{3}^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
n_{2}\mid\widehat{P}(z)\end{subarray}}\frac{\epsilon_{n_{2}}^{-1}}{n_{2}^{2}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\\
p\mid n_{2}\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-2}}{p}\right)^{-1}.

When
n_{2}^{2}n_{3}^{3}\geq N/\widehat{P}(z)=X^{3/2}P(z)
, we get the bound

\displaystyle\ll\frac{\log z}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{n_{3}\leq N^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{1}{n_{3}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left(\frac{X^{3/2}P(z)}{n_{3}^{3}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq n_{2}\leq{(N/n_{3}^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{\log z}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{n_{3}\leq N^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{1}{n_{3}^{3}}\left(\frac{n_{3}^{3/2}}{X^{3/4}P(z)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\ll\frac{\log{z}\,X^{-\frac{3}{4}}}{3^{\pi(z)}P(z)^{\frac{1}{2}}},

which is
O(X^{-\frac{1}{2}})
. Working similarly for the
n_{2}
-sum, separating the range for
n_{3}^{3/2}\leq N/\widehat{P}(z)=X^{\frac{3}{2}}P(z)
, we
get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{p\leq z}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-2}}{p}\right)\sum_{n_{3}\leq N^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{1}{n_{3}^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{2}\mid\widehat{P}(z)\end{subarray}}\frac{\epsilon_{n_{2}}^{-1}}{n_{2}^{2}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\\

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{p\leq z}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-2}}{p}+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-1}}{p^{2}}\right)\left(\zeta(3)+O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{2}{3}}}\right)\right)+O\left(X^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta(3)}{3^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{p\leq z}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-2}}{p}+\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-1}}{p^{2}}\right)+O\left(X^{-1/2}\right).

Replacing in (

), we have

\displaystyle\left(X+O\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP(z))\right)\right)\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\

\displaystyle+O\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP(z))\right).

then
\left(\frac{nm}{\cdot}\right)_{3}
is a non-trivial character of modulo at most
9nm
and using again the GRH, we have that the sum over
\mathfrak{q}
in (
5.5
) is
O(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XN^{2}P(z)^{2})).

Therefore the non-cubic contribution can be bounded by

\ll X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XNP(z))\frac{1}{3^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{1}{n}\ll X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XNP(z))\log N\ll X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{3}(XP(z)).

This concludes the proof.
∎

Our next goal is to prove a result analogous to Proposition
5.2
that can be applied to obtain a lower bound. Since in this case we are interested in characters of order
\ell
with
\ell
prime, we need to consider this more general setting. First, we need to generalize the ideas exposed in Lemma
3.7
.

The characters of order
\ell
are supported on primes
q\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,\ell)
, which are those that split completely in
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{\ell})
, say
q{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]=\mathfrak{q}_{1}\cdots\mathfrak{q}_{\ell-1}
. For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have that

\left\{\chi\,:\,\text{ $\chi$ is a primitive character of conductor $q$}\right\}=\left\{\left(\frac{\cdot}{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}\right)_{\ell},\dots,\left(\frac{\cdot}{\mathfrak{q}_{\ell-1}}\right)_{\ell}\right\},

where the
\ell
th residue symbol is defined for
q\nmid n
by

\left(\frac{n}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}=\omega_{\ell}^{k},\qquad\mbox{ where }\qquad n^{\frac{q-1}{\ell}}\equiv\omega_{\ell}^{k}\,(\mathrm{mod}\,\mathfrak{q}).

Proposition 5.3
.

Assume GRH for Hecke
L
-functions over
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{\ell})
. Let
P_{\ell}(z)=\prod_{p\leq z}p^{\ell-1}=e^{(\ell-1)z+o(z)}
. Let
\epsilon_{p}\in\{\omega_{\ell}^{k}\,:\,k=1,\dots,\ell\}
for each rational prime
p\leq z
, and let

\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)=\{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}(X)\,|\,\mathrm{cond}(\chi)\text{ is prime and }\chi(p)=\epsilon_{p}\text{ for each }p\leq z\}.

Then for
z\ll X^{\frac{1}{6}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}\frac{1}{L(1,\chi)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=\frac{X}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-(\ell-1)}}{p}\right)+O(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{3}(XP_{\ell}(z))).

Proof.

As an application of Perron’s formula, under GRH, it is known that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\sum_{n\leq x}\mu(n)\chi(n)\ll x^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Using this, for large
N
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L(1,\chi)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{\mu(n)\chi(n)}{n}+\int_{N}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{n\leq t}\mu(n)\chi(n)\right)\frac{dt}{t^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{\mu(n)\chi(n)}{n}+O\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right).

By the discussion before the statement of Proposition
5.3
, studying
\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)
is equivalent to studying
\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)
where

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)=\left\{\mathfrak{q}\text{ ideal in }{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]:N(\mathfrak{q})=q\text{ is prime, }q\leqslant X\text{ and }\left(\frac{p}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}=\epsilon_{p}\text{ for each }p\leq z\right\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)

\displaystyle=\#\left\{\mathfrak{q}\text{ ideal in }{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]:N(\mathfrak{q})\leqslant X\text{ and }\left(\frac{p}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}=\epsilon_{p}\text{ for each }p\leq z\right\}

\displaystyle+O\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

We will use the detector

\frac{1}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\mid P_{\ell}(z)\end{subarray}}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\left(\frac{m}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\mathfrak{q}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)$},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We apply (
5.7
) and
\frac{1}{|L(1,\chi)|}\ll\log_{2}\mathrm{cond}(\chi)
under GRH to get

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}\frac{1}{L(1,\chi)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)\left(\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{\mu(n)\chi(n)}{n}+O\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right)\right)

(5.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P_{\ell}(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{\mu(n)}{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\text{ ideal in }{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]\\
\mathfrak{q}\text{ prime }\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\leq X\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{nm}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}\log{N(\mathfrak{q})}+O\left(\frac{X\log X}{N^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}}\right)+O\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log X\log_{2}X\right).

The map
\mathfrak{q}\rightarrow\left(\frac{nm}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}
is a Hecke character over
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{\ell})
that becomes principal when
nm
in an
\ell
th power. In this case, applying the Prime Number Theorem and assuming RH over
{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\text{ ideal in }{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]\\
\mathfrak{q}\text{ prime }\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\leq X\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{nm}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}\log N(\mathfrak{q})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\text{ ideal in }{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]\\
\mathfrak{q}\text{ prime }\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\leq X\end{subarray}}\log N(\mathfrak{q})+O\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\text{ ideal in }{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{\ell}]\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\text{ prime }\\
N(\mathfrak{q})\leq X\\
\mathfrak{q}\mid nm\end{subarray}}1\Bigg)

\displaystyle=X+O_{\ell}\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}{X}+\log{(NP_{\ell}(z))}\right)

\displaystyle=X+O_{\ell}\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP_{\ell}(z))\right),

where we take
N=XP_{\ell}(z)
and we use the assumption that
z\ll X^{\frac{1}{6}}
.
Replacing in (
5.8
), we have

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left(X+O_{\ell}\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP_{\ell}(z))\right)\right)\frac{1}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P_{\ell}(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\
nm=\ell\text{th power}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(n)}{n}.

We write
m=m_{1}m_{2}^{2}\cdots m_{\ell-1}^{\ell-1}
, where the
m_{1},\dots,m_{\ell-1}
are square-free and pairwise coprime and
m_{1},\dots,m_{\ell-1}\mid\widehat{P}(z)
, where we recall that
\widehat{P}(z)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}p
.
Since
n
is square-free, then the only possibility for
nm
to be an
\ell
th power is to have
m_{\ell-1}=n
and
m_{k}=1
for
k=1,\dots,\ell-2
. Therefore (
5.9
) is equal to

\displaystyle\left(X+O_{\ell}\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP_{\ell}(z))\right)\right)\frac{1}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\leq N\\
n\mid\widehat{P}(z)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(n)\epsilon_{n}^{-(\ell-1)}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(X+O_{\ell}\left(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XP_{\ell}(z))\right)\right)}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{p\leq z}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon_{p}^{-(\ell-1)}}{p}\right),

where the last equality follows from the choice of
N
.

The map
\mathfrak{q}\rightarrow\left(\frac{nm}{\mathfrak{q}}\right)_{\ell}
is a non-trivial Hecke character over
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{\ell})
of conductor a multiple of
(nm)
when

nm
is not an
\ell
th power in
{\mathbb{Z}}
, since
n
is square-free and
m
is
\ell
-power free, which follows from the fact that
m\mid P_{\ell}(z)
. Using the GRH again, we have that the sum over
\mathfrak{q}
in (
5.8
) is
O_{\ell}(X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XNP_{\ell}(z)^{2}))
. Therefore, the terms when
nm
is not an
\ell
th power in
{\mathbb{Z}}
contribute

\ll_{\ell}X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XNP_{\ell}(z))\frac{1}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\sum_{m\mid P_{\ell}(z)}\epsilon_{m}^{-1}\sum_{n\leq N}\frac{1}{n}\ll_{\ell}X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2}(XNP_{\ell}(z))\log N\ll X^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{3}(XP_{\ell}(z)).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Eq. (
1.8
) in Theorem
1.5
.

Let us consider the case
\epsilon_{p}=1
for all primes
p\leq z
.
If
3^{\pi(z)}\leq X^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
then using Proposition
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{1\},z)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}\left(1+O(X^{-\epsilon})\right),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{1\},z)}L(1,\chi)\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}\zeta(3)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\left(1+O(X^{-\epsilon})\right).

Applying the triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{1\},z)}|L(1,\chi)|\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg|\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{1\},z)}L(1,\chi)\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}\zeta(3)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\left(1+O(X^{-\epsilon})\right).

We take
z:=\frac{\log X\log_{2}X}{2\log 3}
in order to have
3^{\pi(z)}\leq X^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
. For convenience of notation, we denote

\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}:=\zeta(3)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon_{p}}{p}+\frac{\epsilon_{p}}{p^{2}}\right).

Recall that from (
5.3
), we also have that
|L(1,\chi)|\leq 2\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)
for
\mathrm{cond}(\chi)
sufficiently large.
Let
\delta>0
arbitrary, and assume that the proportion of
\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)
such that
|L(1,\chi)|\geq\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}-\delta
is
\Delta_{\geq\alpha-\delta}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)\\
|L(1,\chi)|<\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}-\delta\end{subarray}}|L(1,\chi)|\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)\\
|L(1,\chi)|\geq\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}-\delta\end{subarray}}|L(1,\chi)|\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\Delta_{\geq\alpha-\delta})\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}(\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}-\delta)+\frac{X}{3^{\pi(z)}}2\log^{2}X\Delta_{\geq\alpha-\delta}

and thus

\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}\leq(1-\Delta_{\geq\alpha-\delta})(\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}-\delta)+2\log^{2}X\Delta_{\geq\alpha-\delta},

and therefore

\frac{\delta}{2\log^{2}X-\alpha_{\epsilon_{p}}+\delta}\leq\Delta_{\geq\alpha-\delta}.

Multiplying
\Delta_{\geq\alpha-\delta}
by the total number of elements in
\mathcal{P}(X,\{\epsilon_{p}\},z)
, which is
X/3^{\pi(z)}\gg X^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}
, we get that there are
\gg X^{\frac{1}{2}}

cubic characters
\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)
with prime conductor such that

\displaystyle|L(1,\chi)|

\displaystyle\geq\zeta(3)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)=\zeta(3)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq e^{\gamma-O(1/z)}\log z\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p>z\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{3}}\right)\geq e^{\gamma}\left(\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X-\log(2\log 3)+o(1)\right).

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.10
.

We fix the values
\epsilon_{p}=\omega_{\ell}^{\frac{\ell-1}{2}}
for all
p\leq z
. Following the proof of
Eq. (
1.8
) in Theorem
1.5
, if
\ell^{\pi(z)}\leq X^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
then applying Proposition
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(X,\{\omega_{\ell}\},z)}\frac{1}{L(1,\chi)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)=\frac{X}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{\omega_{\ell}^{\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}{p}\right)\left(1+O(X^{-\epsilon})\right).

Applying the triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(X,\{\omega_{\ell}\},z)}\frac{1}{|L(1,\chi)|}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg|\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(X,\{\omega_{\ell}\},z)}\frac{1}{L(1,\chi)}\log\mathrm{cond}(\chi)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{X}{\ell^{\pi(z)}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq z\end{subarray}}\left|\left(1-\frac{\omega_{\ell}^{\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}{p}\right)\right|\left(1+O(X^{-\epsilon})\right).

Following the choice of
z:=\frac{\log X\log_{2}X}{2\log\ell}
and similar arguments as in the proof of
Eq. (
1.8
) in Theorem
1.5
, we conclude that
there are
\gg X^{\frac{1}{2}}

\ell
th power characters
\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}(X)
with prime conductor such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|L(1,\chi)|}\geq\prod_{p\leq z}\left|\left(1-\frac{\omega_{\ell}^{\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}{p}\right)\right|=

\displaystyle\prod_{p\leq z}\left(1+\frac{2\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{(e^{\gamma}\log z)^{\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}}{C_{\ell}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\log z}{\sqrt{z}}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(e^{\gamma}(\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X-\log(2\log\ell)))^{\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{\ell}\right)}}{C_{\ell}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\log_{2}X}{\sqrt{\log X}}\right)\right)},

where we have employed the same simplifications as in the proof of Proposition
1.7
.

∎

6.
Strengthening the range in the distribution result via the short Euler product under GRH

Our goal in this section is to obtain the distribution result Theorem
1.4
, by strengthening the range of
\tau
in Theorem
1.3
under GRH. We obtain a range similar to the
\Omega
-result under GRH (as the term
\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X
is achieved).
We remark that Theorem
3.1
plays an integral role in the proof of Theorem
1.3
. Under GRH, we can extend the range of
|z|
in Theorem
3.1
by approximating
L(1,\chi)
with a short Euler product, defined as

L(s,\chi;y):=\prod_{p\leq y}\left(1-\frac{\chi(p)}{p^{s}}\right)^{-1}.

We also define the short random Euler product

L(1,{\mathbb{X}};y)=\prod_{p\leq y}\left(1-\frac{{\mathbb{X}}(p)}{p}\right)^{-1}.

Theorem 6.1
.

Assume GRH for
L(s,\chi)
over
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{3})
.
Let
C>0
,
e^{40}\leq B\leq(\log_{2}X)^{C}
,
z
be a real number and
y:=B\log X\log_{2}X
. Then, uniformly in the region
|z|\leq\frac{\log X\log_{2}X}{e^{37}\log B}
, we have when
X
goes to infinity,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}|L(1,\chi;y)|^{2z}=\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})+O\left(X^{-\frac{23}{100}}\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})\right).

Results of the same quality were obtained for quadratic characters in
[
GS03
]
.

We start with the some auxiliary results.
The following lemma relies on upper bounds for
|\log L(1,\chi_{d})|
proven in
[
GS07
]

for quadratic characters, and extended by Lamzouri
[
Lam10
]
to general
L
-functions.
For the case of quadratic characters, this is analogous to
[
GS03
, Lemma 4.5]
replacing
\sigma=\frac{3}{4}
with
\sigma=\frac{2}{3}
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume GRH for Hecke
L
-functions over
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{3})
.
Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
that is not a cube. Then we have

\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\chi(m)\ll X^{\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon}\exp\left((\log m)^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}\right).

Proof.

Recall from (
3.3
) that

(6.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\chi(m)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in{\mathbb{Z}},d\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
|d|\leq\sqrt{X}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\mathbb{Z}}(d)\chi_{d}(m)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}],\ell\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
(\ell,d)=1\\
N(\ell)\leq\sqrt{X/N(d)}\end{subarray}}\mu_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]}(\ell)\chi_{\ell^{2}}(m)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}],n\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
(n,d)=1\\
N(n)\leq X/N(d\ell^{2})\end{subarray}}\chi_{n}(m).

The function
\psi_{m}:(n)\mapsto\chi_{n}(m)=\left(\frac{m}{n}\right)_{3}
defined on ideals
(n)\subset{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]
(coprime to 3, and where
m\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)
) gives a Hecke character of modulus
9m
. Let

\displaystyle L(s,\psi_{m})=\sum_{(n)}\psi_{m}((n))N(n)^{-s}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}]\\
n\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\end{subarray}}\chi_{n}(m)N(n)^{-s}

be the corresponding Hecke
L
-function.

Using Perron’s summation formula we have, for some
T\geq 1
,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}],n\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
(n,d)=1\\
N(n)\leq Z\end{subarray}}\chi_{n}(m)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1+\epsilon)}f_{m,d}(s)Z^{s}\frac{ds}{s}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{1+\epsilon-iT}^{1+\epsilon+iT}f_{m,d}(s)Z^{s}\frac{ds}{s}+O\left(\frac{Z^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\right),

where
Z:=\frac{X}{N(d\ell^{2})}
and

f_{m,d}(s)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}],n\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
(n,d)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\chi_{m}(n)}{N(n)^{s}}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid d}\left(1-\frac{\psi_{m}(\mathfrak{p})}{N(\mathfrak{p})^{s}}\right)L(s,\psi_{m}).

A special case of Lemmas 4.2 and 4.3 in
[
Lam10
]
implies that if
\sigma_{1}=\min\left(\sigma_{0}+\frac{1}{\log y},\frac{\sigma+\sigma_{0}}{2}\right)
,
where
y\geq 2
,
|t|\geq y+3
and
\sigma>\sigma_{0}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, then

\log L(\sigma+it,\psi_{m})=\sum_{N(n)\leq y}\frac{b_{n}}{N(n)^{\sigma+it}}+O\left(\frac{\log|t|}{(\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{0})^{2}}y^{\sigma_{1}-\sigma}\right),

where

b_{n}=\begin{cases}\frac{\psi_{m}((n))}{k}&\mbox{if }(n)=\mathfrak{p}^{k},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then, for
|t|\leq T
and
y=(\log T)^{\beta}
, we have

\displaystyle|\log L(\sigma+it,\psi_{m})|

\displaystyle\ll y^{1-\sigma}+\frac{\log T}{(\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{0})^{2}}y^{\sigma_{1}-\sigma}

\displaystyle\ll(\log T)^{\beta(1-\sigma)}+(\log T)^{1+\beta(\sigma_{0}-\sigma)}\log_{2}T.

Inserting this estimate after shifting the line of integration in the above Perron’s integral (
6.2
) to
\re(s)=\sigma>\sigma_{0}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, we obtain

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}],n\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
(n,d)=1\\
N(n)\leq Z\end{subarray}}\chi_{n}(m)\ll Z^{\sigma}2^{2\omega(d)}\exp\left((\log T)^{\beta(1-\sigma)}+(\log T)^{1+\beta(\sigma_{0}-\sigma)}\log_{2}T\right)+\frac{Z^{1+\epsilon}}{T}.

We may suppose that
Z\leq N(m)
, since by periodicity
\sum_{N(n)\leq Z}\chi_{m}(n)\ll N(m)
. This implies that
m\geq Z^{\frac{1}{2}}
and hence we can take
T:=m
, since in this case
\frac{Z^{1+\epsilon}}{T}\ll Z^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}
, which is permissible. Also equating
\beta(1-\sigma)
and
1+\beta(\sigma_{0}-\sigma)
, we can choose
\sigma_{0}=\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}
with
\beta\geq 2
.
Therefore, taking
\sigma=\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon
and
\beta=2
, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}],n\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
(n,d)=1\\
N(n)\leq Z\end{subarray}}\chi_{n}(m)\ll Z^{\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon}2^{2\omega(d)}\exp\left((\log m)^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}\right).

Plugging this estimate in (
6.1
) together with
Z=\frac{X}{N(d\ell^{2})}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\chi(m)\ll

\displaystyle X^{\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in{\mathbb{Z}},d\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
d\leq X\end{subarray}}\frac{2^{2\omega(d)}}{d^{\frac{4}{3}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\omega_{3}],\ell\equiv 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)\\
(\ell,d)=1\\
N(\ell)\leq\sqrt{X/N(d)}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(\ell)^{\frac{4}{3}}}\exp\left((\log m)^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle X^{\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon}\exp\left((\log m)^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}\right),

which finishes the proof.
∎

The following is the analogue of Theorem 6.1 from
[
GS03
]
for the case of cubic characters.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume GRH for Hecke
L
-functions over
{\mathbb{Q}}(\omega_{3})
.
Let
2\leq y\leq\exp(\sqrt{\log X})
and let
z
be a real number. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}|L(1,\chi;y)|^{2z}=\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})\left(1+O\left(E(z,y,X)\right)\right),

where
E(z,y,X)
is a positive real number such that
for
y\geq 4(|z|+1)
,

\displaystyle E(z,y,X)\ll X^{-\frac{1}{4}}\exp\left(\frac{16(|z|+1)}{\log(4(|z|+1))}+\frac{120(|z|+1)}{\sqrt{\log X}}+2\log_{2}y+6e^{30}|z|\log\left(\frac{\log y}{\log(4(|z|+1))}\right)\right),

and otherwise,

E(z,y,X)\ll X^{-\frac{1}{4}}\exp\left(\frac{34y}{\log y}\right).

Proof.

We have

(6.3)

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}|L(1,\chi;y)|^{2z}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}=1\\
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{d_{z}(n_{1})d_{z}(n_{2})}{n_{1}n_{2}}S(X;n_{1}n_{2}^{2}),

where

S(X;n)=\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\chi(n),

and
\mathcal{S}(y)
denotes the set of integers whose prime factors are all
\leq y
.
We decompose
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
as
n_{1}=r_{1}r_{2}^{2}r_{3}^{3}r_{4}^{3}
and
n_{2}=s_{1}s_{2}^{2}s_{3}^{3}s_{4}^{3}
, where
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}
are square-free and coprime in pairs, and
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}
are as well. In this decomposition we also have
p\mid r_{4}\Rightarrow p\mid r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}
and
p\mid s_{4}\Rightarrow p\mid s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}
.

Then,
S(X;n_{1}n_{2}^{2})=S(X;r_{1}s_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2}s_{2}r_{3}^{3}s_{3}^{3})
and we rewrite (
6.3
) as

\displaystyle\sum_{r_{1}\in\mathcal{S}(y)}\mu^{2}(r_{1})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(r_{1},r_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(r_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{3}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(r_{3},r_{1}r_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(r_{3})\sum_{s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}(y)}\mu^{2}(s_{1})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(s_{1},s_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(s_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{3}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(s_{3},s_{1}s_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(s_{3})

(6.4)

\displaystyle\times S(X;r_{1}s_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2}s_{2}r_{3}^{3}s_{3}^{3})\sum_{p\mid r_{4}\Rightarrow p\mid r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}\frac{d_{z}(r_{1}r_{2}^{2}r_{3}^{3}r_{4}^{3})}{r_{1}r_{2}^{2}r_{3}^{3}r_{4}^{3}}\sum_{p\mid s_{4}\Rightarrow p\mid s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\frac{d_{z}(s_{1}s_{2}^{2}s_{3}^{3}s_{4}^{3})}{s_{1}s_{2}^{2}s_{3}^{3}s_{4}^{3}}.

If we write
(r_{1},s_{1})=U_{1}
,
(r_{2},s_{2})=U_{2}
, then
r_{1}=U_{1}r_{1,1}
,
s_{1}=U_{1}s_{1,1}
,
r_{2}=U_{2}r_{2,1}
,
s_{2}=U_{2}s_{2,1}
. Then we get that the main term comes from
r_{1,1}s_{1,1}^{2}r_{2,1}^{2}s_{2,1}=\hbox to8.91pt{\vbox to8.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

. Because of coprimality (we have that
(r_{1,1},s_{1,1})=(r_{2,1},s_{2,1})=(r_{1,1},r_{2,1})=(s_{1,1},s_{2,1})=1
) and square-free conditions (
r_{1,1},r_{2,1},s_{1,1},s_{2,1}
are square-free) we get that
r_{1,1}=r_{2,1}=s_{1,1}=s_{2,1}=1
. This gives
r_{1}=s_{1}=r
(say) and
r_{2}=s_{2}=s
(say).
Using Proposition
2.1
, this results in

(6.5)

\displaystyle S(X;r_{1}s_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2}s_{2}r_{3}^{3}s_{3}^{3})=S(X;r^{3}s^{3}r_{3}^{3}s_{3}^{3})=|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|\prod_{p\mid rsr_{3}s_{3}}\alpha_{p}+O\left(3^{\omega(rsr_{3}s_{3})}X^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right),

where the factors
\alpha_{p}
in the Euler product are defined in (
4.1
) and depend on
p\,(\mathrm{mod}\,3)
.
We can now write (for
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}
square-free and coprime)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{4}\geq 1\\
p\mid r_{4}\Rightarrow p\mid r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{d_{z}(r_{1}r_{2}^{2}r_{3}^{3}r_{4}^{3})}{r_{1}r_{2}^{2}r_{3}^{3}r_{4}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{p\mid r_{1}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{d_{z}(p^{3k+1})}{p^{3k+1}}\prod_{p\mid r_{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{d_{z}(p^{3k+2})}{p^{3k+2}}\prod_{p\mid r_{3}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{d_{z}(p^{3k+3})}{p^{3k+3}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{p\mid r_{1}}c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)\prod_{p\mid r_{2}}c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)\prod_{p\mid r_{3}}\left(c_{p,1}(z)-1\right),

where

\displaystyle c_{p,\omega_{3}^{j}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)^{-z}+\omega_{3}^{j}\left(1-\frac{\omega_{3}}{p}\right)^{-z}+\omega_{3}^{2j}\left(1-\frac{\omega_{3}^{2}}{p}\right)^{-z}}{3}.

We remark that
c_{p,\omega_{3}^{j}}(z)\in{\mathbb{R}}
since
z\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

A similar treatment applies to the sum over
s_{4}
. Replacing in (
6
), the contribution of the main term of (
6.5
) to (
6.3
)
is

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|\sum_{r\in\mathcal{S}(y)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(r,s)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{3},s_{3}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(r_{3},rs)=1\\
(s_{3},rs)=1\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(r)\mu^{2}(s)\mu^{2}(r_{3})\mu^{2}(s_{3})\ \prod_{p\mid rsr_{3}s_{3}}\alpha_{p}\prod_{p\mid r}c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)\prod_{p\mid s}c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\prod_{p\mid r_{3}}\left(c_{p,1}(z)-1\right)\prod_{p\mid s_{3}}\left(c_{p,1}(z)-1\right)

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|\prod_{p\leq y}\Bigg(1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\left\{0,1\right\}\\
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\neq(0,0,0,0)\\
k_{1}=1\Rightarrow k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}=0\\
\\
k_{2}=1\Rightarrow k_{1},k_{3},k_{4}=0\end{subarray}}\alpha_{p}c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)^{2k_{1}}c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)^{2k_{2}}\left(c_{p,1}(z)-1\right)^{k_{3}}\left(c_{p,1}(z)-1\right)^{k_{4}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq y\end{subarray}}\left(1+\alpha_{p}c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)+\alpha_{p}c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)+2\alpha_{p}(c_{p,1}(z)-1)+\alpha_{p}(c_{p,1}(z)-1)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq y\end{subarray}}\left(1-\alpha_{p}+\alpha_{p}\left(c^{2}_{p,1}(z)+c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)+c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z}),

where
in the last equality, we have applied the fact that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})

\displaystyle=\prod_{p\leq y}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|1-\frac{\mathbb{X}(p)}{p}\right|^{-2z}\right)=\prod_{p\leq y}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|1-\frac{\mathbb{X}(p)+\overline{\mathbb{X}}(p)}{p}+\frac{\mathbb{X}(p)\overline{\mathbb{X}}(p)}{p^{2}}\right|^{-z}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{p\leq y}\left(1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}\right)

and

(6.6)

\displaystyle c^{2}_{p,1}(z)+c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)+c^{2}_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)=\frac{1}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}+\frac{2}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}.

Now replacing the error term of (
6.5
) in (
6
), and using (
6.6
), the contribution to (
6.3
) is

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\prod_{p\leq y}\left(c_{p,1}^{2}(z)+c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}^{2}(z)+c_{p,\omega_{3}}^{2}(z)\right)

\displaystyle=X^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})\prod_{p\leq y}\left(1+O\left(\frac{2}{p}\right)\right)\ll X^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\log^{2}{y}\;\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z}).

We now proceed to bound the contribution
when

in (

By Lemma

, we have

(6.7)

\displaystyle|S(X;m)|\ll X^{\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon}\exp\left((\log{m})^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}\right)\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}m^{\frac{5}{\sqrt{\log{X}}}},

where the second bound follows by considering the two cases, namely,

\log m\leq\left(\frac{\log X}{25}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}
and

\log m>\left(\frac{\log X}{25}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}
. In the first case,

\exp\left((\log{m})^{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{\log{X}}{25}\right)=X^{\frac{1}{25}}.

In the second case,

\exp\left((\log{m})^{\frac{2}{3}}\right)=\exp\left(\frac{\log{m}}{(\log{m})^{\frac{1}{3}}}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{5\log m}{\sqrt{\log X}}\right)=m^{\frac{5}{\sqrt{\log{X}}}}.

) with
m=r_{1}s_{1}^{2}r_{2}^{2}s_{2}r_{3}^{3}s_{3}^{3}
in (

), the contribution when
