is bounded by

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(r_{2},r_{1})=1\\
(r_{3},r_{1}r_{2})=1\\
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}\;\text{square-free}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\in\mathcal{S}(y)\\
(s_{2},s_{1})=1\\
(s_{3},s_{1}s_{2})=1\\
s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}\;\text{square-free}\end{subarray}}\left|\prod_{p\mid r_{1}}(c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)p^{\frac{5}{\sqrt{\log X}}})\prod_{p\mid r_{2}}(c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)p^{\frac{10}{\sqrt{\log X}}})\prod_{p\mid r_{3}}((c_{p,1}(z)-1)p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}})\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{p\mid s_{1}}(c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)p^{\frac{10}{\sqrt{\log X}}})\prod_{p\mid s_{2}}(c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)p^{\frac{5}{\sqrt{\log X}}})\prod_{p\mid s_{3}}((c_{p,1}(z)-1)p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}})\right|

\displaystyle=X^{\frac{3}{4}}\left|\prod_{p\leq y}\left(1+c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)p^{\frac{5}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)p^{\frac{10}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+(c_{p,1}(z)-1)p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\left(1+c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)p^{\frac{10}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)p^{\frac{5}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+(c_{p,1}(z)-1)p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\prod_{p\leq y}\left(1+|c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+|c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+|c_{p,1}(z)-1|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\right)^{2}.

Now we split the product into the primes that are
\leq 4(|z|+1)
and those that are
>4(|z|+1)
.
When
p\leq 4(|z|+1)
, we use the bound (which is valid for all
p
)

\displaystyle|c_{p,\omega_{3}^{j}}(z)|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left(\left(\left|1-\frac{1}{p}\right|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}+\left(\left|1-\frac{\omega_{3}}{p}\right|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}+\left(\left|1-\frac{\omega_{3}^{2}}{p}\right|^{2}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}+\frac{2}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}.

When
p\geq 4(|z|+1)
we use the bounds

\displaystyle|c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)|\leq\frac{4|z|}{3p},\quad|c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)|\leq\frac{4|z|^{2}}{3p^{2}},\quad|c_{1,p}(z)-1|\leq\frac{4|z|^{3}}{3p^{3}}.

We have

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\prod_{p\leq\min\{y,4(|z|+1)\}}\left(1+|c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+|c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+|c_{p,1}(z)-1|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\min\{y,4(|z|+1)\}<p<y}\left(1+|c_{p,\omega_{3}^{2}}(z)|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+|c_{p,\omega_{3}}(z)|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}+|c_{p,1}(z)-1|p^{\frac{15}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\prod_{p\leq\min\{y,4(|z|+1)\}}p^{\frac{30}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\left(2+\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}+2\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\min\{y,4(|z|+1)\}<p<y}\left(1+\frac{6p^{\frac{30}{\sqrt{\log X}}}|z|}{p}\right).

Now we use that
1\leq\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}+\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}
to get

\displaystyle\left(2+\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}+2\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{z}{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 54\left(1-\alpha_{p}+\frac{\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}+\frac{2\alpha_{p}}{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{-z}\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{2}{p}\right)\right).

Then we get for
4(|z|+1)<y
,

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})\prod_{p\leq 4(|z|+1)}54p^{\frac{30}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{2}{p}\right)\right)\prod_{4(|z|+1)<p<y}\left(1+\frac{6p^{\frac{30}{\sqrt{\log X}}}|z|}{p}\right)

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})\log^{2}y\prod_{p\leq 4(|z|+1)}54p^{\frac{30}{\sqrt{\log X}}}\prod_{4(|z|+1)<p<y}\left(1+\frac{6p^{\frac{30}{\sqrt{\log X}}}|z|}{p}\right)

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})\exp\left(\frac{16(|z|+1)}{\log(4(|z|+1))}+\frac{120(|z|+1)}{\sqrt{\log X}}+2\log_{2}y+6e^{30}|z|\log\left(\frac{\log y}{\log(4(|z|+1))}\right)\right).

For
y\leq 4(|z|+1)
, we have

\displaystyle\ll X^{\frac{3}{4}}\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2z})\exp\left(\frac{34y}{\log y}\right),

since
\log y\leq\sqrt{\log X}
. This finishes the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

We directly use Proposition
6.3
with
y=B\log{X}\log_{2}{X}
and
z=\frac{\log{X}\log_{2}{X}}{e^{37}\log{B}}
, where
e^{40}\leq B\leq(\log_{2}{X})^{C}
to prove the statement. Indeed, from the choice of
y
and
z
, we see that
y\geq 4(|z|+1)
. Therefore, applying the first part of Proposition
6.3
, we have

\displaystyle E(z,y,X)

\displaystyle\ll X^{-\frac{1}{4}}\exp\left(\frac{16\log X\log_{2}X}{e^{37}\log_{2}X\log B}+\frac{120\log X\log_{2}X}{e^{37}\log B\sqrt{\log X}}+2\log_{2}X\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{6e^{30}\log X\log_{2}X}{e^{37}\log B}\log\frac{\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X+\log B}{\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X-37-\log_{2}B+\log 4}\right).

Now notice that

\displaystyle\log\frac{\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X+\log B}{\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X-37-\log_{2}B+\log 4}

\displaystyle=\log\left(\left(1+\frac{\log B}{\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X}\right)\left(1-\frac{37-\log 4+\log_{2}B}{\log_{2}X+\log_{3}X}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\log B}{\log_{2}X},

where for the last inequality we applied the condition
37-\log 4+\log_{2}B<\log B
, which is true provided that
B\geq e^{40}
.

Incorporating this observation, we obtain

\displaystyle E(z,y,X)

\displaystyle\ll X^{-\frac{1}{4}}\exp\left(\frac{17\log X\log_{2}X}{e^{37}\log_{2}X\log B}+\frac{6e^{30}\log X\log_{2}X}{e^{37}\log B}\frac{2\log B}{\log_{2}X}\right)

\displaystyle\ll X^{-\frac{1}{4}}\exp\left(\frac{17\log X}{e^{37}\log B}+\frac{12}{e^{7}}\log X\right)\ll X^{-\frac{23}{100}}.

∎

We now proceed to establish a connection between
L(1,\chi)
and
L(1,\chi;y)
under GRH.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
C>0
, and let
2\leq A\leq(\log_{2}X)^{C}
be a real number and
y:=A^{4}\log X\log_{2}X
. Under GRH, we have

L(1,\chi)=L(1,\chi;y)\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{A\log_{2}X}\right)\right)

for all but at most
X^{\frac{14}{15}}
cubic characters in
\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)
.

To prove this statement we need the following auxiliary result.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
k
be a positive integer.
Under GRH,

\displaystyle\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\bigg|\sum_{y<p\leq z}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}\bigg|^{2k}

\displaystyle\ll X2^{k}k!\left(\sum_{y<p\leq z}\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{k}+X^{2/3+\epsilon}\exp\left((3k\log z)^{\frac{2}{3}}\right)\left(\frac{\log(z/y)}{\log y}\right)^{2k}

\displaystyle\ll X\left(\frac{2k}{y\log y}\right)^{k}+X^{2/3+\epsilon}\exp\left((3k\log z)^{\frac{2}{3}}\right)\left(\frac{\log(z/y)}{\log y}\right)^{2k}.

Proof.

We write

\left(\sum_{y<p\leq z}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}\right)^{k}=\sum_{y^{k}<m\leq z^{k}}\frac{a_{k}(m)\chi(m)}{m},

where

a_{k}(m)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y<p_{1},\dots,p_{k}\leq z\\
p_{1}\cdots p_{k}=m\end{subarray}}1.

This gives us

\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\bigg|\sum_{y<p\leq z}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}\bigg|^{2k}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{k}<m_{1},m_{2}\leq z^{k}\end{subarray}}\frac{a_{k}(m_{1})a_{k}(m_{2})}{m_{1}m_{2}}\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\chi(m_{1}m_{2}^{2}).

Separating the above sum into the cubic and the non-cubic part, and applying Lemma
6.2
, we obtain the following bound

\displaystyle\ll X\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{k}<m_{1},m_{2}\leq z^{k}\\
m_{1}m_{2}^{2}=\hbox to5.21pt{\vbox to5.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\par{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 5.12}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}

{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}

∎

Proof of Proposition
6.4
.

Specializing by
Q=X
,
y=(\log X)^{32}
, and
A=4
in Proposition 2.2 of
[
GS03
]
, we can say that

L(1,\chi)=L(1,\chi;(\log X)^{32})\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\log X}\right)\right)

holds for all but at most
X^{\frac{1}{2}}
characters inside
\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)
.
Observe that for
z>y
,

L(1,\chi;z)=L(1,\chi;y)\exp\left(\sum_{y<p\leq z}\left(\frac{\chi(p)}{p}+O\left(\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\right)\right).

We take
z=(\log X)^{32}
and
y:=A^{4}\log X\log_{2}X
, and it suffices to show that

\bigg|\sum_{y<p\leq z}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{A\log_{2}X}

holds for all but
\ll X^{\frac{14}{15}}
characters inside
\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)
. To do this we dyadically divide the interval
(y,z]
into
J
subintervals of the form
(y_{j},y_{j+1}]
such that
y_{j}:=2^{j-1}y
, where

J=[32\log_{2}X-\log y]
. (We need this to make the quantity
\log(z/y)
small in Lemma
6.5
.)

On the basis of this division, using a version of Chebyshev’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\#\Big\{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)\,:\,\bigg|\sum_{y_{j}<p\leq y_{j+1}}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}\bigg|\geq\frac{1}{A2^{\frac{j}{6}-1}\log_{2}X}\Big\}

(6.10)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}\bigg|\sum_{y_{j}<p\leq y_{j+1}}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}\bigg|^{2k_{j}}\times(A2^{\frac{j}{6}-1}\log_{2}X)^{2k_{j}},

where we take

(6.11)

\displaystyle k_{j}=\frac{\log X}{30\log(A2^{j/3+1})}.

We now apply Lemma
6.5
to bound (
6.10
).
From the first term in the bound of Lemma
6.5
, we get the bound

\displaystyle\ll X\left(\frac{2k_{j}}{y_{j}\log y_{j}}\right)^{k_{j}}(A2^{\frac{j}{6}-1}\log_{2}X)^{2k_{j}}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\ll X\exp\left(k_{j}\left(-\log 30-2\log A-2j/3\log 2\right)\right)

\displaystyle\ll X\exp\left(-2k_{j}\log(5A2^{j/3})\right)\ll X^{\frac{14}{15}},

by (
6.11
) and using the fact
2
2

2

In the estimate (
6.12
) we particularly use that
y=A^{4}\log X\log_{2}X
as opposed to the perhaps more natural choice
y=A\log X\log_{2}X
.
that
y_{j}=2^{j-1}A^{4}\log X\log_{2}X
. Similarly, from the second term in the bound of Lemma
6.5
, we get the bound

\displaystyle\ll X^{2/3+\epsilon}\exp((3k_{j}\log y_{j+1})^{\frac{2}{3}})\left(\frac{\log(y_{j+1}/y_{j})}{\log y_{j}}\right)^{2k_{j}}(A2^{\frac{j}{6}-1}\log_{2}X)^{2k_{j}}

\displaystyle\ll X^{2/3+2\epsilon}\exp(2k_{j}\log(2^{j/6-1}A)+3k_{j}^{\frac{2}{3}}(\log_{2}X)^{\frac{2}{3}}))\ll X^{\frac{4}{5}}.

Therefore we have that

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\bigg|\sum_{y_{j}<p\leq z_{j}}\frac{\chi(p)}{p}\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{A\log_{2}X}

holds for all but at most
X^{\frac{14}{15}}
characters inside
\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)
.
∎

Proposition 6.6
.

For any
r,y>0
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(r;y)

\displaystyle:=\log(\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{2r}))=\sum_{p\leq y}\log E_{p}(r),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r;y)

\displaystyle:=\log(\mathbb{E}(|L(1,\mathbb{X};y)|^{-2r}))=\sum_{p\leq y}\log E_{p}(-r).

Then

(6.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(r;y)=2r\log\log\min\{r,y\}+2r\gamma+\frac{2r\left(C_{\max}(r/y)-1\right)}{\log r}+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)

and

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(r;y)=\log\log\min\{r,y\}+r\gamma+\frac{r\left(C_{\min}(r/y)-1\right)}{\log r}-r\log\zeta(3)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right),

where
2C_{\max}(r/y)=\int_{r/y}^{\infty}\frac{f^{\prime}(u)}{u}du
and
C_{\min}(r/y)=\int_{r/y}^{\infty}\frac{\widetilde{f}^{\prime}(u)}{u}du
and
f(u)
and
\widetilde{f}^{\prime}(u)
are defined by (
4.12
) and (
4.13
) respectively.

Moreover,
2C_{\max}
and
C_{\min}
satisfy

(6.14)

\displaystyle 2C_{\max}(r/y)=2C_{\max}+O(r/y)\quad\text{and}\quad C_{\min}(r/y)=C_{\min}+O(r/y).

Proof.

This is a direct adaptation of the proof of Proposition
4.5
.∎

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

The proof is almost identical to the proof of Theorem
3.1
from Section
4.3
, replacing
L(1,\chi)
by the short Euler product
L(1,\chi;y)
, and keeping track of
y
in the error terms. We define for any
r\geq 1

\displaystyle\phi_{X}(\tau;y)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)\\
|L(1,\chi;y)|\geq e^{\gamma}\tau\end{subarray}}1,

which gives

\displaystyle 2r\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-2r\gamma}}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)}|L(1,\chi;y)|^{2r}.

We apply Theorem
6.1
for
B=A^{4}
and
e^{10}\leq A\leq(\log_{2}X)^{C}
to get

\displaystyle 2r\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt=e^{-2\gamma r}E\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}};y)|^{2r}\right)+O\left(e^{-2\gamma r}X^{-\frac{23}{100}}E\left(|L(1,{\mathbb{X}};y)|^{2r}\right)\right),

uniformly for
r\leq\frac{\log X\log_{2}X}{4e^{37}\log A}
and
y=A^{4}\log X\log_{2}X
(notice that
r<y
). Using (
6.13
) and (
6.14
),
we get

(6.15)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt

\displaystyle=(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}(C_{\max}-1)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{r^{2}}{y\log r}\right)\right).

Let
r_{2}=re^{\delta}
, where
\delta>0
is sufficiently small to be determined later. We apply (
6.15
) with
r_{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt

\displaystyle\leq(\log{r})^{2r}\exp\left(2r(1-e^{\delta})\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\tau}\right)+2re^{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{\delta}{\log r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\times\exp\left(\frac{2re^{\delta}}{\log r}\left(C_{\max}-1\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{r^{2}}{y\log r}\right)\right)\exp\left(2r(1-e^{\delta})\left(\log{\tau}-\log_{2}{r}\right)\right),

which holds uniformly for

\displaystyle r_{2}\leq\frac{\log X\log_{2}X}{4e^{37}\log A}\iff r\leq e^{-\delta}\frac{\log X\log_{2}X}{4e^{37}\log A}.

We now choose
r=r(\tau)
by
\log{r}=\tau-C_{\max}
, and
\delta=\frac{c}{\sqrt{\log r}}

to get

\displaystyle\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt

\displaystyle\leq(\log r)^{2r}\exp\left(\frac{2r}{\log r}\left(C_{\max}-1\right)+O\left(\frac{r}{(\log r)^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{r^{2}}{y\log r}\right)\right)\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right)

for some constant
c
.
Using (
6.15
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\tau+\delta}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt\right)\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right),

and similarly, we can prove that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau-\delta}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{2r-1}\phi_{X}(t;y)dt\right)\exp\left({-\frac{c^{2}r}{(\log r)^{2}}}\right).

Working as in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we get

\displaystyle\phi_{X}(\tau+\delta;y)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\delta\right)+O\left(\frac{e^{\tau}}{y}\right)\right)\right)\leq\phi_{X}(\tau-\delta;y),

and

(6.16)

\displaystyle\phi_{X}(\tau;y)=\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)+O\left(\frac{e^{\tau}}{y}\right)\right)\right)

uniformly in the range

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\log r+C_{\max}\leq\log\left(e^{-\delta}\frac{\log X\log_{2}X}{4e^{37}\log A}\right)+C_{\max}.

To conclude the proof, we know replace
\phi_{X}(\tau;y)
by
\phi_{X}(\tau)
, with the appropriate error terms.
Using Proposition
6.4
, we can write for
y=A^{4}\log X\log_{2}X
and
2<e^{10}\leq A\leq(\log_{2}X)^{C}
,

\displaystyle\phi_{X}(\tau)=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)\\
|L(1,\chi)|\geq e^{\gamma}\tau\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\mathcal{F}_{3}(X)\\
|L(1,\chi;y)|\geq e^{\gamma}\tau\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{A\log_{2}X}\right)\right)\end{subarray}}1+O(X^{-\frac{1}{15}})

\displaystyle=\phi_{X}\left(\tau\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{A\log_{2}X}\right)\right);y\right)+O(X^{-\frac{1}{15}}),

and from (
6.16
), we have

\displaystyle\phi_{X}\left(\tau\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{A\log_{2}X}\right)\right);y\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}+O(\tau/(A\log_{2}{X}))}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{A\log_{2}X}\right)+O\left(\frac{e^{\tau}}{y}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{A}\right)+O\left(\frac{e^{\tau}}{y}\right)\right)\right).

Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle\phi_{X}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2e^{\tau-C_{\max}}}{\tau}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{A}\right)\right)\right),

since
\frac{e^{\tau}}{y}\ll\frac{1}{A^{4}\log A}
.
This completes the proof for
\phi_{X}(\tau)
. A similar estimate holds for
\psi_{X}(\tau)
.

∎
